The hook ALWAYS exits 0 (an EXIT trap, and deliberately no set -e: fr exits 2 on every refusal and exit 2 from a Stop hook IS a block). Silent, exit 0, on: no binding for the session, fr missing, jq missing, fr erroring, the migration gate refusing, an fr too old for --idle, fr killed, fr hanging (20s watchdog, test bounded), exit 3 with no/garbage/non-object output, a complete answer that says not idle, idle JSON under exit 0, a missing position or next command, an unusable binding, an unusable payload, a subagent's stop. Every stub case asserts the stub was reached, so none passes by never looking. Mutation-verified, 12 mutants.<br>[super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L332>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L373>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L388>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L428>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L438>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L464>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L477>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L598>) |
| Ending a turn while a dispatched executor is still working is allowed | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#L159>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L293>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | The subtle legitimate stop: on Claude Code a background executor's return arrives as a notification, so a turn that ends while a unit is HELD is correct. Pinned at both levels: the predicate says held (unit), and the hook is silent against the real fr with a unit dispatched (int). A second, harness-side witness was found on the 2.1.278 binary and is honoured too: a non-empty background_tasks or session_crons in the Stop payload means the session is paused and will be woken, so the guard does not act. That field's POPULATED shape was read from the binary's schema, not captured — stated in tests/fixtures/hooks/NOTE.md.<br>[super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#L159>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L293>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L579>) |
| On OpenCode, an idle run is continued by re-injecting the next command when the session goes idle | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | not implemented | LIVE-ONLY BY NATURE - stays not-implemented, deliberately. The handler exists (packages/fr-opencode-plugin/src/idle.ts, wired as the existing plugin's event hook) and bun test covers it - 50 tests, 10 mutants killed: exactly one promptAsync per run position on session.idle, silent otherwise, child sessions left alone, never throws. But every one of those tests drives a FAKE client. That a plugin-originated prompt on session.idle actually EXECUTES in a live OpenCode session - that the event fires for a TUI session at the moment a turn ends, that promptAsync is callable from inside an event hook, that the injected text is acted on, and that it does not collide with the operator typing - is shown by none of them, and that is the claim this row makes. By the gh#494 standard it needs a live run; until then parity.yaml says opencode: partial with a scope_note saying why. The adapter cannot BLOCK a stop, only continue the session - a weaker tier than the Claude Code guard, declared as such rather than implied to be parity. VERSION CORRECTION (phase 6, carried into prose and parity.yaml in phase 7): the event and the endpoint were verified in @opencode-ai/sdk TYPE DEFINITIONS on the authoring machine, of versions 1.17.15, 1.1.27 and 1.0.23 - all three carrying EventSessionIdle {properties.sessionID}, Session.parentID, and session.promptAsync -> /session/{id}/prompt_async with body.parts and query.directory. 1.18.31 is the opencode BINARY's version and is NOT an SDK copy on disk here; the earlier claim "verified in the installed 1.18.31 SDK types" (spec section 4.G table, parity.yaml scope_note) was wrong about WHICH artifact was read and both are now corrected. The design is unaffected - every version found carries both - but a verification is only worth its provenance. |
| The idle guard acts at most once per cursor position, so a failing advance cannot trap a session in a loop | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#L361>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L485>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | The loop breaker. fr emits an opaque position (run, cursor, step state, gate, each unit's state and attempt count — no timestamp, so a failing advance that only re-stamps a record is not progress). The hook remembers the position it last acted on beside the session binding, WRITES AND READS IT BACK before blocking, and does not block at all if it cannot: a second stop at the same position passes, and it acts again once the cursor moves. Independent brakes: stop_hook_active must be literally false (the CAPTURED continuation payload is let through with no memory at all; a build that drops the field silences the guard rather than un-braking it), and Claude Code's own CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP (default 8, read from the binary). fr isolation detach deletes the memory with the binding.<br>[super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#L361>) [super-fr:test_run_idle.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_idle.py#L380>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L485>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L499>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L510>) [super-fr:test_run_idle_guard.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_run_idle_guard.py#L548>) |

| super-fr's phase-executor agent ships to OpenCode as a real registered subagent: the canonical Claude Code agent is mirrored into .opencode/agent/ with OpenCode's own frontmatter, and drift between the two is a CI failure. | [super-fr:2026-09-19-opencode-subagent-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-19-opencode-subagent-dispatch-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_opencode_agent_mirror.py>) ~~API~~ [INT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_install_opencode_agents.py>) ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Phases 1-3 shipped the generator (canonical_agents(), the CLOSED tools:->permission: translation, drift detection) and its unit coverage; CI-pinned by the unit suite plus the install integration tests. Live half, re-verified independently by the orchestrator: the shipped .opencode/agent/ mirror copied into a bare directory carrying no opencode.json and no super-fr install registers fr-phase-executor plus all three tier agents as (subagent) on opencode 1.18.31 — Test Plan §5 item 1, and the portability the in-repo run cannot show.<br>[super-fr:test_opencode_agent_mirror.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_opencode_agent_mirror.py>) [super-fr:test_install_opencode_agents.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/integration/test_install_opencode_agents.py>) |
| An fr-goal run on OpenCode hands a plan phase to the fr-phase-executor subagent and gets a genuine child session — parented, separately costed — rather than executing the phase inline in the orchestrator's own context. | [super-fr:2026-09-19-opencode-subagent-dispatch-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-19-opencode-subagent-dispatch-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | skipped | LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-21 — the post-merge run this row was owed. A real headless /fr-goal on OpenCode (github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra) dispatched a phase: opencode.db child session ses_f3b00175… with parent_id set, agent=fr-phase-executor-mechanical (the tier the plan phase declared), on the answered model, with its own usage and its 32 tool calls recorded under the child rather than the orchestrator. Evidence: the gh#494 phase-6 and gh#498 phase-4 journals (p6-live-evidence, p4-live-evidence). STILL skipped, correctly: reproducing it needs a live opencode binary and a model, neither available in CI. SCOPE, unchanged from before and still true: the headline proves a genuine dispatch, not that tiering resolved — the tiering claim lives in opencode-tier-binding-reaches-dispatch. The headline cannot be corrected because there is no fr acceptance edit verb; that is #522, since folded into #510. CORRECTION: an earlier version of this note said a follow-up for that verb had been filed. It had not — #522 found the claim false. #522 became that follow-up and is now folded into #510. Found by the same run, and filed: #537 (fr's cursor names the wrong holder for an OpenCode dispatch, and cannot name the tier agent that ran) and #538 (fr-goal never asked the model-per-tier question it is gated on). DISCRIMINATING RUN, 2026-09-22 (the model-level proof the earlier runs could not give): the 2026-09-21 run's orchestrator and child both ran on gpt-5.6-terra and all three tiers were bound to it, so its session-row model could not tell a tiered dispatch from an untiered one; its proof rested on the agent NAME alone, for one tier. This run bound FOUR distinct models - mechanical github-copilot/gpt-5.6-luna, standard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra, hard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-sol, orchestrator github-copilot/claude-sonnet-5 - in a throwaway local repo (fr-tier-proof, local bare origin, no forge resource) whose plan declared phases 1/2/3 as mechanical/standard/hard. opencode.db, root ses_f37014a55ffePrm0GvcXNde25v (build, claude-sonnet-5), three parented children in phase order: ses_f36fd32e5ffeLnEhUiVhc8dhwj fr-phase-executor-mechanical gpt-5.6-luna $0.0132; ses_f36fb6911ffeFuz6aUuFfsbWjz fr-phase-executor-standard gpt-5.6-terra $0.1326; ses_f36f9f94dffeXlvlEQ6t1mCyBs fr-phase-executor-hard gpt-5.6-sol $0.3867 (run total $1.71). All six assertions PASS: three children; declared tier -> tier agent in order; each child on its tier's binding (read from the installed agent files, last written before the run and stable through it); NO child on the session model; child models pairwise distinct; bindings themselves distinct, i.e. the test could have failed. That closes the gap the scope note above records: tiering resolves to the bound MODEL per tier, not merely to a tier-named agent. #537 reproduced in the same run: fr's cursor records agent_type super-fr:fr-phase-executor for all three, never the tier agent that ran - though it does record the three child session ids, matching opencode.db exactly, so the two stores can be joined by session id. Still skipped: live-only, no CI level. PAID RUN (2026-09-23, #564 Test Plan step 3, fr 4.17.2, real feature #457 -> PR #586, orchestrator build on github-copilot/gpt-6-luna). Cursor vs opencode.db: both phases (plan tier mechanical) were dispatched to fr-phase-executor-mechanical on github-copilot/claude-haiku-4.5 = the binding; each cursor holder id equals its child session id exactly; both review-phase reviewer= ids equal the dispatched general child sessions exactly (on gpt-6-luna); every child is parented to the root; deliver carries tests=<log>. The cursor's agent_type still reads super-fr:fr-phase-executor, never the tier agent (#537). The question batch WAS asked and the turn ended; the operator answered. But brainstorm was resolved with no --emitted spec= and no --answered-by operator, so the cursor records answered_by=agent and no spec, and fr accepted it (#587). Row stays skipped (live-only). |

Witness decision u1 (spec journal) made them literally the same claim: the witness IS the open attempt, and "the unit is running" is lifecycle state everywhere else, so the two rows could only ever move together and a reader had no way to tell which one to trust. THIS row is canonical. The other is NOT deleted - the matrix ships add / set-status / check and no delete or edit verb (checked: fr acceptance --help), and removing a row by hand is exactly the hand-edit the acceptance-matrix rule forbids - so it remains as a pointer back here. | A run pushed mid-phase and picked up on another host shows the orphaned holder, refuses a double dispatch, and recovers via --abandoned | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | ~~UNIT~~ ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | not implemented | LIVE-ONLY BY NATURE - stays not-implemented, re-confirmed at phase 7. The mechanism's unit half landed in phase 4 and is genuinely covered: the refusal now says the holder was dispatched from ANOTHER session and that its cost is not observable from here, and names fr run claim --abandoned (tests/unit/test_run_cli.py::test_advance_refusing_another_sessions_holder_says_it_cannot_see_it, with test_advance_refusing_its_own_sessions_holder_says_nothing_about_sessions pinning that the ORDINARY refusal does not acquire that sentence), and the window is refused for an attempt this session did not dispatch (tests/unit/test_run_telemetry.py::test_the_window_is_refused_for_an_attempt_this_session_did_not_dispatch). But this row's claim is TWO REAL HOSTS: a cursor pushed mid-phase and pulled on another machine whose transcripts (~/.claude/projects) and session bindings (~/.cache/fr/sessions) never travelled with the branch, and whose executor may have died with its host. No unit test can be two machines - every such test simulates absence by not writing a file, which is the thing being tested. Flipping it on the unit evidence would report a live claim as verified. What closes it: a live demonstration, Test Plan item 16's sibling. Phase 7 shipped the operator-facing half in fr-goal SKILL.md section 5 ("Picking the run up somewhere else?"), so the recovery is now documented where an operator meets it - documentation is not verification, and the row stays red until the walk happens. |
| An attempt dispatched from another session or host is never assigned this session's subagent costs, and its cost shows as not observable | [super-fr:2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/docs/superpowers/implemented/specs/2026-09-20-unit-record-unification-design.md>) | [UNIT](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_telemetry.py>) ~~API~~ ~~INT~~ ~~UI~~ | CI · per-PR | Unit level landed in phase 4. test_the_window_is_refused_for_an_attempt_this_session_did_not_dispatch builds spec 4.D.1 exactly (host B's own session holds exactly ONE unrelated subagent inside the window) and pins BOTH defences: the transcript is looked up in the RECORDED session's directory, and select_for_attempt refuses the window unless same_session. Non-vacuity: the same call with same_session=True DOES return the stranger. test_an_attempt_with_no_recorded_session_never_borrows_the_window and test_a_process_with_no_session_of_its_own_measures_nothing_by_window cover the two not-knowing cases; test_status_says_a_cost_from_another_session_is_not_observable_from_here covers the rendering. Mutation-verified: deleting the same_session gate fails 3 tests, making locate_session ignore the recorded session fails 2.<br>[super-fr:test_run_telemetry.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_telemetry.py>) [super-fr:test_run_cli.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_run_cli.py>) |

Element content: a <script> title, a </script> body and a markup label render only escaped, the single script element carries no facts text, and inline markup escapes first then allows only code and bold. Attribute: test_a_title_cannot_break_out_of_the_data_search_attribute parses the page and finds no on* attribute on any element and the literal quote inside data-search. Href: test_a_non_https_url_produces_no_href (javascript:, data:, HTTPS://, whitespace-led). Masthead, tier and pattern: test_hostile_text_in_every_context_renders_escaped (theme, tier title, tier description, pattern title, skipped repo and reason, unviewed reason, scope) and test_hostile_pattern_ids_render_escaped_even_past_the_loader. Mutation proof 2026-09-21: dropping each esc() in render.py, or loosening _safe_url to strip/lower or to accept anything, turned exactly the matching test red (11 of 11). check's human output escapes rich markup (r7, test_triage_check.py).<br>[super-fr:test_triage_render.py](<https://github.com/derio-net/super-fr/blob/main/tests/unit/test_triage_render.py>) |

Commands that address an existing workspace route by that recorded mode; a legacy state's mode is inferred. FR_ISOLATION_TARGET selects the backend only for up and gc, and fails closed there. The integration test was verified red against the pre-change source (journal p2-t4-not-characterization). ## § The sharp line — where drift can hide

### Skipped — verification exists but does not run in CI (backfill owed)

- **acceptance-pipeline-rows** — Rows are born at brainstorm (presented with defenses), linked in plans, flipped at execution; self-review fails unlinked plans. _Mechanics unit-pinned per-PR; end-to-end toy walk (Test Plan item 4) owed post-merge._
- **acceptance-added-since** — Mid-flight row additions are diffable against a base ref and defended in the delivered PR body. _CLI diff ci-pinned; the PR-body defense is process — demonstrated in PR #352, not automated._
- **acceptance-nag-channels** — Session start surfaces open debt (capped), fr status carries the summary, and the weekly digest issue upserts and closes at zero. _Hook, digest formatter, and branch/PR/main Actions summary scaffold are ci-pinned; live weekly upsert/close remains unverified until the first scheduled run._
- **isolation-suite-lifecycle** — Operator can init a devcontainer profile and run isolated work: up → exec → status → down, with PR-aware down refusal. _CLI + scaffold unit-pinned; the live worktree+container walk was proven at the 2.4.0 release, not in CI._
- **opencode-skills-in-repo** — An operator can git clone super-fr and use its fr-* skills directly from OpenCode with no install step. _Unit test guards mirror drift (CI). Test Plan step 1 walked post-merge (2026-07-09, fresh isolation devcontainer on merged main, no prior install step): `opencode debug skill` in the checkout discovered all 9 `fr-*` skills, each resolving to `.opencode/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` in the worktree — confirms the "clone and use, no install" claim. Not automated in CI (requires a live `opencode` binary) — backfill to `ci` owed if that becomes scriptable._
- **opencode-skills-installer** — An operator can run scripts/install.sh and get super-fr's skills available globally in OpenCode. _Unit test guards the install.sh wiring (CI). Test Plan step 2 walked post-merge (2026-07-09, fresh isolation devcontainer, scratch $HOME, merged main): `scripts/install.sh` with `OPENCODE_SKILLS_INSTALL=1` delivered all 9 skills to `$HOME/.config/opencode/skills/`, and `scripts/install.sh --uninstall` removed them cleanly (dir emptied). Not automated in CI — backfill owed if that becomes scriptable end-to-end._
- **opencode-commands-in-repo** — An operator can type /fr-goal (and the other 8 mirrored fr-* skills) as a real OpenCode slash command inside a super-fr clone, with no install step. _Unit test guards mirror drift (CI) — tests/unit/test_tripwire_opencode_commands_sync.py passes (2026-07-08). Test Plan steps 1-2 walked post-merge (2026-07-08, fresh clone of merged main) — `opencode debug config` resolved all 9 generated commands (fr-goal, fr-plan, ... — zero install), and a live `opencode run --command fr-progress` call (opencode/north-mini-code-free, no arguments) correctly invoked the skill tool with name=fr-progress and got a sensible response — confirms the empty-$ARGUMENTS case end to end. Not automated in CI (requires a live opencode binary + model call) — backfill owed if that becomes scriptable, same pattern as the sibling opencode-skills-in-repo row._
- **opencode-commands-installer** — An operator can run scripts/install.sh and get /fr-goal etc. available as OpenCode slash commands globally, with clean --uninstall removal. _Unit test guards the install.sh wiring (CI) — tests/unit/test_install_copies_opencode_commands.py passes (2026-07-08). Live-verified twice — pre-merge (2026-07-08, scratch HOME, VK_INSTALL_SKIP_PREFLIGHT=1) and re-confirmed post-merge as Test Plan step 3 (2026-07-08, fresh clone of merged main, real preflight passing — no skip needed) — both times full scripts/install.sh delivered all 9 commands byte-identical to .opencode/commands/, and --uninstall removed them cleanly with no collateral damage. Not automated in CI (requires real filesystem/HOME side effects) — backfill owed if that becomes scriptable, same pattern as the sibling opencode-skills-installer row._
- **multibackend-gitlab-tracking** — An operator can run fr apply against a GitLab-backed repo and see phases rendered/observed/diffed/applied as GitLab Issues with correctly-shaped GitLab labels (color, length). _LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-19 against a self-hosted GitLab (gh-486): fr apply rendered, observed, diffed and applied two phases as GitLab Issues with correctly-shaped labels (#-prefixed 6-hex, applied once), re-ran clean, and propagated a state change then re-converged. Transcript in the PR. The row was previously 'ci' for a capability broken FOUR independent ways, none findable by unit test: the missing mandatory ref parameter; the Issue web_url being /-/work_items/N, which fr stored and then refused to parse (crashing observe on every re-apply, on gitlab.com too); ensure_labels aborting the whole apply on a 409 for a label that already existed, a tolerance its own docstring had claimed since 2026-07-09; and the body diff never converging because GitLab strips the rendered trailing newline. 'skipped', NOT 'ci': CI cannot reach a private instance, so the live half is hand-run per-PR. The unit half now asserts the REQUEST and the real captured error vocabulary, so the arg-blind 'lambda args:' mocks that hid all of this are gone. LIVE 2026-09-24 (multi-backend P9.T1.S1), a self-hosted GitLab (host/user/project redacted; private scratch project, deleted after): every RealGlabClient operation fr apply/observe uses passed when driven directly (ensure_labels create+converge, colour round-trip, create_issue returning /-/work_items/N, view, label add/remove, comment, list_linked_prs via related_merge_requests, pr_status_by_url, file_exists/list_dir/read_file, close). related_merge_requests is eventually consistent (empty right after the MR, populated seconds later). NOT walked: an end-to-end `fr apply` on a GitLab-backed plan, so the status stays skipped._
- **fr-goal-subagent-per-phase** — fr-goal implements each plan phase in a sequenced subagent (dependency order) that receives a journal+spec+phase brief, builds the single shared branch, writes discoveries/findings back to the journal, and returns a structured result — no new operator gate. _Skill wiring + agent def + allowlist helper unit-pinned per-PR; the live per-phase dispatch was demonstrated by this feature's own fr-goal run, not yet a CI integration test (backfill owed)._
- **fr-goal-journal-handoff-complete** — A fresh subagent given only fr journal render + spec + its phase yaml has every input needed to implement the phase; a dropped finding is caught by fr journal check. _render/check unit-pinned; the completeness bar (a fresh subagent implements from journal+spec+phase alone) was met live in this feature's own run — a CI integration test dispatching fr-phase-executor is owed._
- **fr-debugging-journal** — fr-debugging records its investigation (repro, hypotheses, ruled-out, root-cause, fix) to journals/<debug-slug>.md via the fr journal debug scope as the investigation proceeds, and derives its PR body via fr journal render --scope debug; existing debugging/*.md prose is left untouched. _SKILL.md wiring (debug-scope add/render, hypothesis/ruled-out trail) content-pinned per-PR; the live debugging walk that records to a debug journal is demonstrated-not- automated (backfill owed). First live walk recorded at journals/debug/2026-09-08-session-unbind-hook-cancelled.md — repro, three ruled-out entries, root-cause and finding all written via `fr journal add --scope debug` as the investigation proceeded, PR body derived from `fr journal render --scope debug`. Kept at skipped rather than flipped to ci because one demonstration is not automation, and the backfill owed here is a harness that runs the walk._
- **hermes-fr-goal-delegation** — A full fr-goal run executes inside Hermes, delegating each phase via delegate_task with a journal-fed brief and resolving models via --harness hermes.. _Wiring unit-tested (fr-goal names delegate_task; no guessed hermes model bindings ship, so fr-goal asks the operator per tier on first run). End-to-end run is post-merge manual Test Plan item 6 (toy feature in real Hermes)._
- **cli-starts-from-a-clean-install** — The fr CLI starts from a clean tool install using only its declared dependencies, so a consumer who installs the release can run it.. _Spec 8.0. 4.0.0 shipped a direct import of click - typer's TRANSITIVE dep, not one fr declares - so the installed CLI could not start while the full local suite stayed green; only CI's uv-tool path caught it. The tripwire pins that one import; nothing yet asserts the installed CLI actually runs. Test Plan 8.0 covers it manually until smoke-suite-automated lands._
- **gitlab-contents-reads-live** — An operator's GitLab-backed repo can be read through fr's contents API — file existence, file text, and directory listing — against a live instance whose default branch is not 'main'. _Unit half in CI asserts the REQUEST (every contents read pins ref=HEAD, captured via a recording double rather than an arg-discarding mock). Live half hand-run 2026-09-19 against a self-hosted GitLab whose default branch is 'master', which is why ref=HEAD rather than a branch name: file_exists True for a present file and False for an absent one, list_dir real entries, read_file real text — all four broken before this PR. 'skipped' because CI cannot reach a private instance._
- **gitlab-selfhosted-host-targeting** — An operator whose GitLab is self-hosted reaches it by declaring 'backend: gitlab' alone; 'host:' overrides, and a host declared for a backend fr cannot target says so. _All three clauses now hold. (a) 'backend: gitlab' ALONE suffices — verified live from a checkout with no host: key: host_for derived gitlab.internal.example from the git remote and client_for built a RealGlabClient carrying it. (b) 'host:' overrides the derived value. (c) A host declared for a backend fr cannot target warns once, on next use rather than at scaffold time. Also closes the bare-URL case the config tiers cannot reach: backend_for_url reads the forge off the URL path, so the VK bridge stops resolving a self-hosted GitLab MR to the GitHub adapter. KNOWN LIMIT, stated in the PR: pr_state's Issue auto-close still targets the SaaS host, blocked on closer's 3-arg arity, not on backend resolution. 'skipped' because the live half is hand-run._
- **opencode-subagent-dispatch** — An fr-goal run on OpenCode hands a plan phase to the fr-phase-executor subagent and gets a genuine child session — parented, separately costed — rather than executing the phase inline in the orchestrator's own context.. _LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-21 — the post-merge run this row was owed. A real headless /fr-goal on OpenCode (github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra) dispatched a phase: opencode.db child session ses_f3b00175… with parent_id set, agent=fr-phase-executor-mechanical (the tier the plan phase declared), on the answered model, with its own usage and its 32 tool calls recorded under the child rather than the orchestrator. Evidence: the gh#494 phase-6 and gh#498 phase-4 journals (p6-live-evidence, p4-live-evidence). STILL skipped, correctly: reproducing it needs a live opencode binary and a model, neither available in CI. SCOPE, unchanged from before and still true: the headline proves a genuine dispatch, not that tiering resolved — the tiering claim lives in opencode-tier-binding-reaches-dispatch. The headline cannot be corrected because there is no fr acceptance edit verb; that is #522, since folded into #510. CORRECTION: an earlier version of this note said a follow-up for that verb had been filed. It had not — #522 found the claim false. #522 became that follow-up and is now folded into #510. Found by the same run, and filed: #537 (fr's cursor names the wrong holder for an OpenCode dispatch, and cannot name the tier agent that ran) and #538 (fr-goal never asked the model-per-tier question it is gated on). DISCRIMINATING RUN, 2026-09-22 (the model-level proof the earlier runs could not give): the 2026-09-21 run's orchestrator and child both ran on gpt-5.6-terra and all three tiers were bound to it, so its session-row model could not tell a tiered dispatch from an untiered one; its proof rested on the agent NAME alone, for one tier. This run bound FOUR distinct models - mechanical github-copilot/gpt-5.6-luna, standard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra, hard github-copilot/gpt-5.6-sol, orchestrator github-copilot/claude-sonnet-5 - in a throwaway local repo (fr-tier-proof, local bare origin, no forge resource) whose plan declared phases 1/2/3 as mechanical/standard/hard. opencode.db, root ses_f37014a55ffePrm0GvcXNde25v (build, claude-sonnet-5), three parented children in phase order: ses_f36fd32e5ffeLnEhUiVhc8dhwj fr-phase-executor-mechanical gpt-5.6-luna $0.0132; ses_f36fb6911ffeFuz6aUuFfsbWjz fr-phase-executor-standard gpt-5.6-terra $0.1326; ses_f36f9f94dffeXlvlEQ6t1mCyBs fr-phase-executor-hard gpt-5.6-sol $0.3867 (run total $1.71). All six assertions PASS: three children; declared tier -> tier agent in order; each child on its tier's binding (read from the installed agent files, last written before the run and stable through it); NO child on the session model; child models pairwise distinct; bindings themselves distinct, i.e. the test could have failed. That closes the gap the scope note above records: tiering resolves to the bound MODEL per tier, not merely to a tier-named agent. #537 reproduced in the same run: fr's cursor records agent_type super-fr:fr-phase-executor for all three, never the tier agent that ran - though it does record the three child session ids, matching opencode.db exactly, so the two stores can be joined by session id. Still skipped: live-only, no CI level. PAID RUN (2026-09-23, #564 Test Plan step 3, fr 4.17.2, real feature #457 -> PR #586, orchestrator build on github-copilot/gpt-6-luna). Cursor vs opencode.db: both phases (plan tier mechanical) were dispatched to fr-phase-executor-mechanical on github-copilot/claude-haiku-4.5 = the binding; each cursor holder id equals its child session id exactly; both review-phase reviewer= ids equal the dispatched general child sessions exactly (on gpt-6-luna); every child is parented to the root; deliver carries tests=<log>. The cursor's agent_type still reads super-fr:fr-phase-executor, never the tier agent (#537). The question batch WAS asked and the turn ended; the operator answered. But brainstorm was resolved with no --emitted spec= and no --answered-by operator, so the cursor records answered_by=agent and no spec, and fr accepted it (#587). Row stays skipped (live-only)._
- **run-dispatch-harness-neutral** — The dispatch holder record is written identically on Claude Code and OpenCode, each recording its own harness and agent id. _not-implemented -> skipped on 2026-09-21: driven live post-merge on the installed fr 4.12.0, one throwaway one-phase /fr-goal per harness (Claude Code and OpenCode; ids and the OpenCode model redacted in the evidence file). Both wrote the same attempt keys, each DETECTING its own harness with no --harness flag, and both refused a second advance with exit 2 naming the holder. skipped, not ci: a manual live run no CI job can repeat. The run surfaced five defects (C1-C5 in the evidence file), all fixed in #527 - notably that on OpenCode the child session id is only returned when the blocking task tool returns, which fr now states in the dispatch-holder-identity parity row. run-pickup-on-another-host and run-idle-reprompt-opencode were NOT exercised and stay not-implemented._
- **goal-executor-refuses-tick** — A phase executor facing a dispatch step it has no tool for reports a BLOCKER and leaves the step unticked. _Moved not-implemented → skipped, NOT ci, deliberately: verification now exists but it is prose-level only, and calling that ci would be an overclaim. test_skill_tokens.py::test_fr_phase_executor_names_the_dispatch_refusal and ::test_fr_execute_names_the_dispatch_refusal pin that the BLOCKER-not-a-tick contract is PRESENT on both surfaces — the canonical agent file and the fr-execute skill. They cannot show an executor OBEYED it: that behaviour is agent-side and not unit-testable, and the only thing that could demonstrate it is a live run against a plan containing a dispatch step — which #428's lint now refuses to let anyone author. So the enforcement is a pair: the lint stops the step being written (executably pinned by goal-agentic-dispatch-purity and goal-dispatch-lint-precision) and this prose catches a step that predates the lint or arrives from outside. On Hermes the prose IS the whole enforcement — nothing there denies a further delegate_task — which is why the contract lives in the skill and not only in the Claude Code agent file. Backfill owed: a live executor run against a pre-lint dispatch step._
- **triage-skill-claude-code** — An agent on Claude Code can follow fr-triage end to end for a repo and an org. _Walked live post-merge 2026-09-22 (Test Plan 11) on Claude Code 2.1.278 / Opus 5: repo scope as a SYNC of an OpenCode-written ranking (1 new issue judged, 0 existing changed) and org scope over live derio-net (18/19 repos collected, 1 skipped with its reason, 112 issues rendered, willikins ranked by operator scope). Both found fr-triage from a plain ask. Evidence: plan journal walk-claude-code-repo and walk-claude-code-org. Not in CI: needs a live harness and model._
- **triage-skill-opencode** — An agent on OpenCode, which has no Artifact tool, can follow fr-triage end to end for a repo. _Walked live post-merge 2026-09-22 (Test Plan 12) on OpenCode with github-copilot/gpt-5.6-terra, a non-Claude model: first-run repo triage (33 issues judged into a new judgements.yaml, board rendered, no forge writes); fr-triage discovered from a plain ask. It surfaced a skill defect (the YAML example teaches invalid YAML), fixed separately. Evidence: plan journal walk-opencode. Not in CI: needs a live OpenCode session._
- **scaffold-jvm-toolchain** — --tool java/maven yields a container where java -version (at the project's detected version) and mvn -v succeed. _gh#574: --tool java/maven map to the java devcontainer feature (installMaven). The version is detected from version files, then the root pom.xml; unit tests pin this. Live walk 2026-09-23 on arm64 (journal p6-live-574, recorded in the PR): with pom.xml maven.compiler.release 17, scaffold printed 'java 17 (from pom.xml maven.compiler.release)'. In the container, mvn -v reported Maven 3.9.16 on Java 17.0.20.1 and java -version reported 17.0.20.1. The status is skipped because that live container walk is not in CI._
- **fr-goal-main-session-cost** — The operator can see what the main session cost per pipeline step, on Claude Code and OpenCode. _Unit-pinned (both readers, _complete_step hook, fr run cost); the cross-harness live check (spec Test Plan 15) is owed post-merge, so not ci yet — phase-1 review p1-f1._

### Not implemented — no test or surface yet

- **dispatch-unit-declared-by-shape** — The granularity dispatch creates work items at (whole run, per plan phase, or per target repo of a spec) is declared by the workflow shape, not hardcoded by the dispatcher.. _Spec 4.E. Phase 8 landed the mechanism (fr_dispatch.item_graph.build_items is the single builder and honours all three units) and Phase 9 widened tick's source to Plan \| SpecMeta \| None. Phase 12 removed the hardcoding this note used to name: the bridge no longer rides tick's built-in default but resolves each plan's own shape from _meta.yaml's `workflow:` key (bridge_cli.py -> workflow_for_plan -> tick(workflow=...)), so the dispatcher genuinely reads granularity off the shape. STAYS not-implemented because only one of the three declared units is reachable that way — unit:phase. A unit:run shape needs a run id that plan discovery has no source for, and a unit:spec shape needs a SpecMeta that no shipped caller ever produces (discover_plans yields Plans, fr apply takes a plan dir; SpecMeta appears in no bridge or CLI call site). An operator therefore still cannot SELECT run- or spec-granularity through a shipped path. The remaining gap is pinned rather than described: tests/integration/test_bridge_shape_binding.py::test_a_plan_naming_a_run_unit_shape_is_refused_for_want_of_a_run_id asserts the loud per-plan failure inside the bridge's I9 boundary (daemon survives, nothing dispatched at the wrong granularity). Flip when a shipped caller can dispatch a run- or spec-unit shape end to end._
- **multi-repo-spec-fanout** — Dispatching a spec fans out one work item per target repo named in its Implementation Plans table, each carrying a parent reference to the spec.. _Spec 4.E/4.F. Phase 9 landed the fan-out end to end at library level — build_items._spec_items decomposes a SpecMeta into one item per distinct target repo (manual/placeholder rows skipped by design, malformed Repo cells reported as failures), tick accepts a SpecMeta source, and tests/integration/test_spec_fanout.py drives a 3-row fixture through tick with a fake runner, each item parented to the spec-level id. Phase 12 added the plan->shape binding this note previously named as the blocker, but that binding does NOT unblock this row: `workflow:` lives in a PLAN's _meta.yaml, while a unit:spec shape decomposes a SpecMeta. No shipped caller ever produces a SpecMeta dispatch source — the bridge's discover_plans yields Plans and fr apply takes a plan dir (grep: SpecMeta appears nowhere in bridge_cli.py or packages/fr/src/fr/commands/). Spec fan-out therefore remains fake-runner-only and operator-unobservable. Flip when a shipped entry point dispatches a spec — a spec target for `fr apply --to`, or discovery that yields specs alongside plans._
- **smoke-suite-automated** — The post-merge smoke suite runs automatically against a clean install rather than depending on an operator remembering to run it.. _Spec 8.0 is written as one scriptable command per step precisely so it can be automated. Not built in this PR - operator-requested follow-up. Each step is currently manual; the class of defect it guards (a release whose CLI cannot start) is invisible to the in-workspace suite. Review r5-b5 sharpened WHY this row matters: step 8.0.3 (`fr workflow check --all`) used to print "no workflow shapes found" and exit 0, so the manual smoke suite REPORTED SUCCESS on a host where no shape was installed at all — the exact state that step exists to detect. `--all` now exits 1 on zero shapes (fr.commands.workflow_cmd, tests/unit/test_workflow_check.py), so automating this row would now catch the defect rather than inherit a green false negative. Until it is automated the whole suite still depends on an operator running it._
- **every-artifact-declares-its-version** — Every artifact fr generates declares the version it was written for, and a file with no stamp reads as version 1 rather than as an error.. _Spec 3.A. Per-artifact stamps chosen over a repo manifest because a manifest cannot describe a half-migrated tree. Phase 1 lands the registry and a reader/writer per kind, and the unit refs cover the second half of the claim in CI — an absent stamp reads as version 1, not an error, for all five kinds, and `fr validate artifacts` now fails an unreadable or newer-than-this-fr stamp on every kind. RE-EVALUATED IN PHASE 7 AND DELIBERATELY LEFT RED. The FIRST half is still unmet and nothing in this plan changed it — only plans carry a stamp in practice, while `fr journal add`, `fr run start` / `fr run adopt` (save_run_state) and the spec writer all emit unstamped files. Absence READING as 1 is not the same claim as every generated artifact DECLARING its version, and flipping this row on that equivalence would be exactly the overclaim the row exists to prevent. What closes it — the generators write the stamp, which is owed at the moment a kind's current_version first moves past 1, in the same PR that must widen that kind's extra=forbid model with an optional defaulted schema_version field (finding f-closed-world-models-reject-a-stamp; obligation now standing in .claude/rules/artifact-versioning.md)._
- **run-pickup-on-another-host** — A run pushed mid-phase and picked up on another host shows the orphaned holder, refuses a double dispatch, and recovers via --abandoned. _LIVE-ONLY BY NATURE - stays not-implemented, re-confirmed at phase 7. The mechanism's unit half landed in phase 4 and is genuinely covered: the refusal now says the holder was dispatched from ANOTHER session and that its cost is not observable from here, and names fr run claim --abandoned (tests/unit/test_run_cli.py::test_advance_refusing_another_sessions_holder_says_it_cannot_see_it, with test_advance_refusing_its_own_sessions_holder_says_nothing_about_sessions pinning that the ORDINARY refusal does not acquire that sentence), and the window is refused for an attempt this session did not dispatch (tests/unit/test_run_telemetry.py::test_the_window_is_refused_for_an_attempt_this_session_did_not_dispatch). But this row's claim is TWO REAL HOSTS: a cursor pushed mid-phase and pulled on another machine whose transcripts (~/.claude/projects) and session bindings (~/.cache/fr/sessions) never travelled with the branch, and whose executor may have died with its host. No unit test can be two machines - every such test simulates absence by not writing a file, which is the thing being tested. Flipping it on the unit evidence would report a live claim as verified. What closes it: a live demonstration, Test Plan item 16's sibling. Phase 7 shipped the operator-facing half in fr-goal SKILL.md section 5 ("Picking the run up somewhere else?"), so the recovery is now documented where an operator meets it - documentation is not verification, and the row stays red until the walk happens._
- **run-idle-reprompt-opencode** — On OpenCode, an idle run is continued by re-injecting the next command when the session goes idle. _LIVE-ONLY BY NATURE - stays not-implemented, deliberately. The handler exists (packages/fr-opencode-plugin/src/idle.ts, wired as the existing plugin's event hook) and bun test covers it - 50 tests, 10 mutants killed: exactly one promptAsync per run position on session.idle, silent otherwise, child sessions left alone, never throws. But every one of those tests drives a FAKE client. That a plugin-originated prompt on session.idle actually EXECUTES in a live OpenCode session - that the event fires for a TUI session at the moment a turn ends, that promptAsync is callable from inside an event hook, that the injected text is acted on, and that it does not collide with the operator typing - is shown by none of them, and that is the claim this row makes. By the gh#494 standard it needs a live run; until then parity.yaml says opencode: partial with a scope_note saying why. The adapter cannot BLOCK a stop, only continue the session - a weaker tier than the Claude Code guard, declared as such rather than implied to be parity. VERSION CORRECTION (phase 6, carried into prose and parity.yaml in phase 7): the event and the endpoint were verified in @opencode-ai/sdk TYPE DEFINITIONS on the authoring machine, of versions 1.17.15, 1.1.27 and 1.0.23 - all three carrying EventSessionIdle {properties.sessionID}, Session.parentID, and session.promptAsync -> /session/{id}/prompt_async with body.parts and query.directory. 1.18.31 is the opencode BINARY's version and is NOT an SDK copy on disk here; the earlier claim "verified in the installed 1.18.31 SDK types" (spec section 4.G table, parity.yaml scope_note) was wrong about WHICH artifact was read and both are now corrected. The design is unaffected - every version found carries both - but a verification is only worth its provenance._
- **triage-skill-hermes** — An agent on Hermes can follow fr-triage end to end. _Born at brainstorm; decision d4 chose not to walk Hermes. Stays not-implemented until someone does._

_Statuses: ci / scheduled cannot drift silently; skipped and not-implemented are CI warnings gated by the backfill rule; failing fails CI._
